1. **deepseek-cls-repo-s123**: ANALYZING status, training completed on 2026-05-17. Needs result extraction. The artifact comparison doc shows eval results (74.93% accuracy at seq8192), but these might have been extracted separately. I need to spawn a worker to check if eval was already done, and if not, run eval and update registry.

2. **deepseek-cwm-repo-s123**: DONE(failed) in registry, but cron says PID 668680 still running. This was the training that completed (wall_clock ~22.68h), but the registry shows failed. 

The PID mismatch is telling—668680 in the cron reconcile is likely a separate eval process that started after the original training (PID 542901) finished, so the cron got registered with this newer PID instead. Looking at the reconcile output, I can see there are multiple crons tracked, including one for deepseek-cwm-repo-s42-epoch2-eval, which suggests eval jobs are being spawned as separate processes with their own PIDs.

The real issue is that deepseek-cwm-repo-s123 with PID 668680 is actively running on GPU 3—it's using 17699 MB of memory at 50% utilization—but the registry incorrectly marks it as DONE(failed). This is a state mismatch where the process is still executing while the registry thinks it's already completed.

For actionable next steps, I need to spawn a worker to extract the deepseek-cls-repo-s123 training results and run evaluation if necessary. The CWM s123 registry issue is something I should flag but the process will finish on its own. Looking at the cls results from the comparison document, I'm seeing accuracy of 74.93% at seq8192 eval with an F1 macro of 53.94%, though I need to verify whether this was the final variant-level evaluation or just an intermediate check.

Since I need to maintain at least 2 workers running, I should spawn one to check the seetacloud eval results and update the registry once confirmed, and potentially queue up preparation for the Qwen3-8B TRAJ eval command to run when that training finishes—though the cron should handle that automatically.

Actually, I'm better off using the second worker slot to investigate the CWM s123 registry inconsistency and figure out what's actually running on GPU 3 with that PID, then fix the registry accordingly.
